## 🟢 Sixty-seventh pass (`p862`, 2026-11-17) — **`861-G3` DISCHARGED: both named legs were run, both returned, and the `North America` leg produced non-US data for the first time in seven passes**

🔴 **`861-G3` said two of five regional legs under-cover their own territory** — `North America`
returns US-only results, `EMEA` returns `0` African data — 🔴 **and that the cause is the QUERY,
not the closed vocabulary.** 🟢 **Its acceptance test was one pass that runs
`AI education Kenya Nigeria South Africa 2026 regulation` and `AI education Canada Mexico 2026
spend` and reports what each returns, including nothing.** 🟢 **Both were run on `2026-11-17`.
Both returned. The remedy — name the jurisdiction, not the region — holds for a second time.**

| Leg | Run as | Returned |
|---|---|---|
| 🟢 **`North America`** | `AI education Canada Mexico 2026 spend adoption` | 🟢 **Canadian federal programme figures and Mexican spend forecasts — `0` US-only drift** |
| 🟢 **`EMEA`/Africa** | `AI education Kenya Nigeria South Africa 2026 regulation` | 🟢 **Three national positions with dates, one of them disputed between sources** |
| 🟡 `APAC` | `AI education APAC 2026 adoption regulation players` | 🟡 **Returned, but see the placement error below** |
| 🟢 `LATAM` | `AI education LATAM 2026 adoption regulation players` | 🟢 **The strongest adoption data of any region this pass** |

🔴 **And the APAC leg produced a placement error worth recording.** It returned the **UAE Ministry
of Education's `25` generative-AI prohibitions for schools (February `2026`)** as an APAC result.
🔴 **The UAE is `EMEA` under this base's closed vocabulary.** 🟢 **It is filed below under EMEA.**
🔵 **A regional query returns what a search engine thinks the region is; the `region` field must
be set from the jurisdiction named in the source, never from the leg that found it.**

## Opportunities by region

### 🟢 North America

🟢 **Canada, with figures** — its national *AI for All* strategy launched June `2026`, targeting a
rise in business AI use from roughly **`12%` to `60%` by `2034`**; sources put its size between
**`$2B` in new funding and `~$8B`** once existing programmes are counted 🔴 *(disputed — the
spread is the story)*. 🟢 **Education-specific line items**: **`$162M` over five years** to Mitacs
for **`10,000`** co-funded AI work placements for students and recent graduates, and **`$30M`** to
CanCode for AI and coding teaching in K-12. 🔴 **The gap that creates the work: Canada ranked
`44th` of `47` countries on AI training and literacy** in a KPMG–University of Melbourne study.
🔵 **Opportunity: the money is in PLACEMENT and TEACHER/WORKFORCE CAPABILITY, not in classroom
tooling** — a studio offer shaped as curriculum plus assessment plus placement tracking fits the
instruments that exist.

🟢 **Mexico, with figures** — enterprise AI software and services spend forecast at
**US `$776M` in `2026`**, inside total ICT spend of **MX `$547B`, growing `4.1%`**. 🟡 **Edtech
spend reported at US `$4.4B` (`2024`) rising to US `$14.4B` (`2033`)** 🔴 *(IMARC, reached through
a secondary blog — treat as indicative)*. 🔴 **No official figure for PUBLIC AI-education spend in
Mexico was found; recorded as a measured absence, not as zero.**

🔵 **Supply note from this pass:** the open-source examination tier found today is Portuguese and
does not serve Mexico. 🔴 **Mexico's own examination vocabulary — `EXANI`, `COMIPEMS` — has never
been queried here** → `862-G2`.

### 🟢 EMEA

🟢 **Africa, covered for the first time after `861-G3`:**
- 🟢 **Kenya** — National AI Strategy `2025`–`2030` launched `27 March 2025`; a **draft AI Bill
  published `19 February 2026`** organising governance in six parts; a technical committee was to
  finalise a national AI and emerging-technologies policy by **June `2026`**.
- 🟡 **Nigeria** — National AI Strategy **still a draft** (published August `2024`). 🔴 **The
  education-specific action is enforcement, not strategy: the data-protection regulator required
  tertiary institutions to produce proof of compliance within `21` days.**
- 🔴 **South Africa** — AI is governed through **existing** law (POPIA, Consumer Protection Act,
  ECTA). A draft National AI Policy proposed **STEAM-focused curricula and community AI centres**.
  🔴 **Its status is DISPUTED between sources: one records the draft as abrogated `27 April 2026`,
  another as published for public consultation `10 April 2026`.** 🔵 **Published as a conflict
  rather than resolved by preference — the gazette is the only settlement.**

🟢 **Gulf** — the **UAE Ministry of Education issued `25` generative-AI prohibitions for schools in
February `2026`**, covering age suitability, privacy, academic integrity and examination rules.
🔵 **A prohibitions list is a procurement specification in disguise: it states exactly what a
compliant classroom tool must not do.**

🟢 **UK / EU** — the UK DfE's product safety standards for generative AI in schools took effect
**January `2026`**; **Ofqual has published principles for AI use in high-stakes marking**. 🔵 **The
Ofqual item is the directly actionable one for this base's assessment shelf: it makes human-in-the-
loop marking a regulatory requirement, not a design preference, and the permissive autograding tier
[`p861` shelved](../agents/top.md) is the component layer under it.**

🔴 **Supply note:** Spain's examination vocabulary returns `7`, `60` and `3` repositories, with
nothing above one star in two of the three → `T225`. 🔵 **An Iberian assessment engagement is a
build, and should be priced as a build.**

### 🟡 APAC

🟢 **Institutional movement is well evidenced; national regulation is not.** The **World Bank's
Global EdTech Policy Academy on AI** runs in **Seoul, `31 August`–`4 September 2026`**; **Cambridge
English** convened Asia-Pacific education leaders in **Kuala Lumpur, April `2026`**; **EdTech Hub**
has launched AI-in-education briefs for South-East Asia. 🟡 **Adoption is uneven** — one survey puts
virtual-tutor adoption at **`30%`** across its cohort — and 🟡 **an Ipsos study finds lower support
for banning AI in schools in Asian markets than elsewhere**. 🟡 **Teacher training is the named
lever, with `34,000` lead teachers reported in training through `2026`** 🔴 *(the source does not
name the jurisdiction — recorded with that defect visible)*.

🔴 **DECLARED GAP, and it is this pass's largest regional hole**: the leg returned **nothing on
China, India, Japan, Korea or Singapore national AI-education frameworks**, which is most of the
region's policy weight. 🔵 **By `861-G3`'s own logic the remedy is to name those jurisdictions in
the query; it was not run this pass** → `862-G3`.

### 🟢 LATAM

🟢 **The adoption data here is the strongest of any region, and it diverges sharply from
readiness** — the Digital Education Council's `2026` LATAM higher-education survey (with
Tecnológico de Monterrey's Institute for the Future of Education) reports **`92%` of students and
`79%` of faculty actively engaging with AI**, while 🔴 **only `30%` believe their institution has
responded effectively.** 🟢 **UNESCO IESALC's survey of `200` institutions across `19` countries
(September `2026`) describes adoption as *neither uniform nor evenly distributed* and finds a
particular gap between private and public universities.** 🔴 **A regional regulatory framework was
NOT found, and one survey states plainly that formal institutional AI policies are lacking.**

🟢 **Active institutional players to compose with, not compete against**: UNESCO IESALC and UNU-IAS
(the governance mapping), the **IDB** (a snapshot of `193` real AI-in-education initiatives),
**EdTech Hub with Fundación Ceibal** (a new regional partnership), and **Tec de Monterrey**.
🟡 **Market sizing for the region is weak** — one report puts *Rest of Latin America* AI-in-education
at **US `$18.2M` (`2024`) → `$36M` (`2029`), `12.1%` CAGR** 🔴 *(a narrow residual definition; it
excludes Brazil and Mexico and should not be quoted as the regional market)*.

🟢 **AND THIS PASS ADDS THE SUPPLY SIDE, which the surveys do not cover.** 🔴 **The `92%`/`30%`
split is a governance and assessment-integrity problem, and the open-source material to attack it
in Portuguese now exists and is measured**: a human-scored essay corpus
([`uol-redacoes-xml`](https://github.com/gpassero/uol-redacoes-xml), Apache-2.0), an ENEM evaluation
harness ([`gpt-4-enem`](https://github.com/piresramon/gpt-4-enem), MIT), an item extractor
([`enem-extractor`](https://github.com/diaslui/enem-extractor), MIT) and a question API
([`enem-api`](https://github.com/yunger7/enem-api), 🔴 GPL-2.0). 🔴 **The constraint is equally
measured: `12` of the `26` addresses in that vein cannot be used at all, and the four most popular
are among them** → `T223`.

🔵 **The engagement shape this supports**: *assessment integrity under high AI usage* — score an
institution's own essays against a human-marked Portuguese gold set, publish the agreement rate,
and let the governance policy follow a measured number instead of a prohibition. 🟢 **Brazil can
start from corpora; Spanish-speaking LATAM cannot, and that is now a measured difference rather
than an assumption** → `T221`, `T225`.

### 🟢 Global

🟡 **Market size, with the disagreement left visible**: US **`$10.6B` (`2026`) → `$42.48B`
(`2030`), `41.5%` CAGR** against a second estimate of **`$9.58B` growing `>30%`/yr**. 🔴 **Both are
vendor-research figures and they differ by more than a year of growth; this base quotes the range,
never a point.** 🟢 **Adoption: `86%` of education organisations report using generative AI**
(Microsoft `2025`, via Faculty Focus). 🔴 **Evidence: only `45%` of the most-used tools have
published research meeting an ESSA evidence tier** — 🔵 **the single most useful number in this
section, because it names the deliverable a studio can sell: proof.**

🟢 **Policy**: the US Department of Education released **`$169M` in FIPSE awards** including support
for responsible AI use; **Ofqual** published marking principles; the **OECD Digital Education
Outlook `2026`** holds that AI aids learning only where pedagogy stays in control. 🟡 **HolonIQ's
`2026` snapshot** reports governments expanding early-career pathways and universities deepening
industry-aligned microcredentials. 🔴 **Reported as FADING by the same analyses: blockchain
credentialing, full-classroom VR, and the chatbot-as-tutor pitch.**

## Opportunities by region

### North America

🟢 **The opening: this is the region where the ASSESSMENT buyer is both compelled and
integrated, and `P95` is written for it.** 🟢 **`9` of this pass's `28` byte-read rows are placed
here** — and the three that matter commercially are published by the incumbents themselves:
[`gradescope-utils`](https://github.com/gradescope/gradescope-utils) is **MIT, holder
`Turnitin, LLC`**; [`autograding-grading-reporter`](https://github.com/classroom-resources/autograding-grading-reporter)
is **MIT, holder `GitHub`**; [`StudentSim`](https://github.com/microsoft/StudentSim) is **MIT,
holder `Microsoft Corporation`**. 🔵 **Three vendors whose products a North American university
already licenses have each published a permissive seam. The engagement is an integration, not a
migration.**

🟢 **The compellable calendar, quoted by state and bill as `p797`'s rule requires:**
**North Carolina `SL 2026-41`** — state model policy by `2026-12-31`, local school-unit policies
by `2027-06-30`, teacher training by `2028-06-30`; **Ohio** districts must adopt an AI framework
by `2026-07-01`; **Maryland, Idaho, Oklahoma and Virginia** carry `2026` laws requiring BOTH state
guidance and mandatory district-level policy adoption; **New York `A 9190`** would restrict
classroom AI to grade `9` and above with diagnostic and special-education exceptions;
**Mississippi** embeds AI literacy for all incoming undergraduates from Fall `2026`.
🟡 **Federal: the Department of Education published an Office of Educational Technology framework
in March `2026` with K-12 guidance in April, and the House committee advanced the
`K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026`** — 🔴 final status not confirmed by this leg.
🟡 **California remains the outlier worth watching: the `Parents & Kids Safe AI Act` ballot
initiative (OpenAI and Common Sense Media) would bind education deployments through CHILD-SAFETY
law rather than education law** — a different compliance surface with a different owner at the
client.

🔴 **The named gap, now SIXTH pass: no spend figure for Canada or Mexico, and this pass's leg
reproduced the cause exactly.** 🟢 **The mandated query says *North America* and the channel
returns US-only results — every item above is a US state or US federal instrument, and the leg
returned Canadian material only for WORKPLACE AI, not classrooms.** 🔵 **The fix is still the
cheap one: name the countries in the query. It has not been run.**

### EMEA

🟢 **The opening: the clock is the product, and this pass's leg put a NEW instrument on it.**
🔴 **The EU AI Act as amended by the `2026` Digital Omnibus classifies education AI used for
admissions, GRADING, steering learning and detecting cheating as high-risk** — 🟢 **which names
this pass's entire tier, clause by clause.** 🔵 **A marking system in Europe is not a product
decision; it is an Annex III deployment.**

🔴 **And the dates conflict across today's sources, which must be published rather than
smoothed.** 🟢 This base carries `Regulation (EU) 2026/1744` with standalone Annex III at
`2027-12-02` and embedded systems at `2028-08-02`; 🟢 **one of today's guides reproduces exactly
those two dates — a fifth independent corroboration of `T939`** — 🔴 **while another states that
high-risk requirements apply from August `2026`.** 🔵 **The split that pays is unchanged and is
the commercial point: Art. `50` transparency and the AI-literacy duty are ALREADY live (literacy
since February `2025`), while the expensive conformity work has its slack. The cheap work is
late; the expensive work is not.**

🟢 **`3` rows placed here this pass, and both named institutions are teaching institutions rather
than ministries:** [`uhafner/autograding-github-action`](https://github.com/uhafner/autograding-github-action)
(Munich University of Applied Sciences) and [`jameskermode/mograder`](https://github.com/jameskermode/mograder)
(University of Warwick, by `CITATION.cff`). 🔵 **Europe's assessment supply is written by lecturers
for their own courses — small, MIT, and maintained. That is a partner list, not a competitor list.**

🟢 **Live demand signals from today's leg:** the UK's Department for Education published
generative-AI **product safety standards for education in January `2026`** — 🔵 **a procurement
checklist a studio can build to, and the most directly sellable instrument in the region;**
Instructure is building an AI study coach into **Canvas**; Pearson is acquiring **Workera** for AI
skills verification; OpenAI reports education partnerships in **Estonia, Slovakia, Greece and
Italy**; QS research puts weekly generative-AI use at `67%` of academics and `62%` of students.
🟢 **Gulf: the UAE's KHDA has issued explicit responsible-AI guidance to private schools.**

🔴 **Two gaps, both named rather than left silent.** 🔴 **AFRICA: this pass's EMEA leg returned no
African adoption or regulatory data at all — the source itself says so. `EMEA` as a single bucket
is now measurably hiding a continent, and the remedy is one query naming African jurisdictions.**
🔴 **And the standing spend figure (`$2.11B`–`$5.86B`, a `2.8×` spread) was not narrowed today.**

### APAC

🟢 **The opening: APAC is the region where education is named in STATUTE rather than in guidance,
and this pass's leg sharpened the list.** 🟢 **Vietnam's `Decision No. 33/2026/QD-TTg` identifies
high-risk AI systems across SIX sectors with education among them**, under the Law on Artificial
Intelligence in force `2026-03-01` with pre-deployment registration; 🟢 **South Korea's AI Basic
Act is the region's first comprehensive AI statute**; 🟡 **Australia relies on existing law plus
voluntary guidance, while TEQSA compels an institutional generative-AI action plan from every
provider**; 🟡 **Japan is betting on voluntary guidelines and industry self-regulation**;
🔴 **Indonesia's AI regulation was still unfinalised as a Presidential Regulation at the time of
this leg.** 🟡 **Vietnam's MOET `Decision 2422` reportedly sets `12` periods per class per year of
AI content for grades `1`–`12` — 🔴 single-tracker testimony, flagged by the source itself, and
not corroborated here.**

🟢 **The attitude data is the region's commercial tell, and it moved in both directions.** Ipsos'
`2026` Education Monitor finds support for BANNING AI in schools rising in Japan (`21%` → `29%`),
Australia (`47%` → `52%`) and South Korea (`27%` → `31%`), and FALLING in Indonesia
(`30%` → `23%`). 🔵 **"APAC is permissive" is no longer a usable generalisation: the mature
markets are hardening while the large emerging one is opening.** 🔵 **A regional pitch deck must
be re-cut per jurisdiction, and the Ipsos series is the cheapest way to do it.**

🟢 **Supply: `1` row placed, and it is placed properly** —
[`skt09/IndiGrader`](https://github.com/skt09/IndiGrader), MIT, a zero-trust autograding platform
built for CLOSED-NETWORK deployment, placed APAC from `cse.iitm.ac.in` in its own README.
🔵 **`8★`, and the star count is a fact about the channel rather than about the work: it is the
only artefact in `28` that was designed for the exam-hall conditions three APAC statutes imply.**

🔴 **And `T935`'s trap reproduced for a THIRD consecutive pass: this pass's APAC leg returned the
UAE's KHDA — an EMEA instrument — as an APAC result.** 🔵 **The regional query channel leaks
across the Gulf boundary reliably enough that it should now be treated as a known defect of the
leg, not as a surprise.** 🔴 **`0` procurement documents for India, Singapore or Australia, sixth
pass: a TENDER-ACCESS gap, not a discovery gap.**

### LATAM

🟢 **The opening: the region's constraint is governance capacity, and today's leg put a
denominator on it that is sharper than the supply story.** 🔴 **UNESCO, as cited by this pass's
leg: only `19%` of institutions have an APPROVED internal regulation.** 🔵 Read beside the figures
this base already holds — `87%` use, `9%` evaluation, `8%` any AI budget and `4%` among PUBLIC
universities (UNESCO IESALC, `200` institutions / `19` countries), and the Digital Education
Council LATAM survey with Tecnológico de Monterrey at `92%` of students and `79%` of faculty using
AI against `30%` who think their institution has responded effectively — 🟢 **the sellable product
in LATAM is the POLICY INSTRUMENT and the evidence trail, not the model.**

🟢 **The regulatory map as today's leg returns it.** 🔴 **No unified regional framework for AI in
higher education.** 🟢 **Chile leads: a National AI Policy since `2021` and a bill in discussion.**
🟡 **Brazil and Colombia have national strategies but no education-sector regulation.** 🔴 **Mexico
has neither a national law nor mandatory guidelines.** 🟢 **General AI bills are under debate in
Argentina, Brazil, Chile, Colombia, Costa Rica, Ecuador, Mexico, Panama, Peru and Uruguay**, and
two are education-specific: 🟢 **an Argentine bill making AI education mandatory, progressive and
transversal at initial and primary level, and a Peruvian bill on AI training in basic education.**
🔵 **Several analyses expect `2026`–`2030` LATAM instruments modelled on the EU's risk-based
approach — which means `P94`'s architecture is forward-compatible here, and a European
Annex III-shaped build is the right thing to propose.**

🔴 **A channel failure to publish, not to hide: today's leg did NOT return Brazil's
`Parecer CNE/CP nº 15/2026`** — the instrument this base already holds, homologated `2026-11-10`,
with its `12`-month clock for policies, governance instruments and CONTRACTS, its stage-gate
through `5º ano` and its two assessment rules (`T67`, `T68`). 🔵 **The strongest LATAM instrument
in this base is invisible to the mandated Spanish-language leg. That is a fact about the leg.**

🔴 **And three figures from this leg are named here so that no later pass quotes them.** 🔴 **A
secondary site attributes `71%` weekly AI use by Latin American university students to Anthropic
and a `$412M` investment to Colombia's MinEducación; neither is traceable to a primary source, and
the same page cites a `Ley 2626 de 2026` with inconsistent dates.** 🟢 **Recorded as
UNVERIFIED-DO-NOT-QUOTE rather than dropped silently, because a dropped figure comes back.**

🔴 **Supply: `0` rows placed in LATAM this pass, and it is an UNRUN LEG rather than a nil.** 🟢 The
Spanish and Portuguese legs that WERE run (`L6`, `L7`, `34` and `53` results) were pointed at the
tutoring vocabulary, and they reproduce `T940` exactly: of `60`+ in-language addresses returned,
🔴 **`0` above one star and almost all are coursework, personal portfolios or NotebookLM study
notebooks.** 🔵 **The assessment vocabulary in Spanish and Portuguese — `corrección automática`,

### Global

🔵 **What belongs to no region — and the three gaps this pass leaves open.**
🔴 **The heading above is bare on purpose.** `gate-region-headings` enforces a CLOSED vocabulary on the `###` children of this block, and every pass since `p854` has written `### Global — <subtitle>`, which the gate reads as `OUT-OF-VOCAB` **and** as `Global` being MISSING. 🟢 **`p861`'s block is the first that passes both halves**; the subtitle moved into the body, where it was always readable.

🟢 **The commercial fact that crosses every region, measured in one batch today:** 🔴 **the
assessment PLATFORM layer is copyleft (`3` of `4` GPL/AGPL) and its COMPONENT layer is permissive
(`18` of `18` MIT, Apache-2.0 or BSD)** — `T218`. 🟢 **So the same architectural decision applies
in North America, EMEA, APAC and LATAM alike: inherit the platform under its own terms, build the
AI layer beside it under yours.** 🔵 **With exactly one escape hatch, and it is worth knowing by
name: [`Submitty`](https://github.com/Submitty/Submitty) is BSD-3-Clause with `1,123` forks.**

🔴 **Gap 1 — Africa is unmeasured and was named as such by this pass's own source.**
🔴 **Gap 2 — Canada and Mexico have no spend figure for a sixth pass, with the cause (a region-named
query returning US-only results) now reproduced twice.**
🔴 **Gap 3 — `15` of this pass's `28` rows are honestly unplaced, because an affiliation is stated
in only `8` trees and `gh api users/{login}` is refused in this session.**

| 🌏 **APAC** | 🔴 **`0`** | 🔴 **And this is an INSTRUMENT gap, not a supply nil.** Two rows with Chinese-language READMEs and Chinese holder names (`Li-Evan/BloomTutor` `312★`, `flysheep-ai/education-skills` `125★`) were shelved **`Global`** because `T935` forbids reading language as region and `gh api users/{login}` is `403` in this session, so origin cannot be resolved. 🔵 **The supply exists; the placement channel does not** |

### Demand, regulation and adoption — `CITED, NOT READ` from the four regional legs run `2026-11-16`

🔴 **Every figure in this section comes from a WebSearch summary. None was read at source, none is
reproducible from this repository, and market sizings disagree by a factor of two.**

| Scope | Figure, as cited | Source as cited |
|---|---|---|
| AI in education, `2025` | ~**`USD 6.4B`** | IMARC |
| AI in education, `2026` → `2030` | ~**`$10.6B`** → **`$42.48B`**, `41.5%` CAGR | Research and Markets, via a secondary summary |
| AI in education, `2025` → `2032` | `USD 5.37B` → `USD 12.30B` | unnamed firm, via summary |
| AI in education, `2025`–`2030` | growth of `USD 3.37B` at **`45%`** CAGR | Technavio |
| EdTech overall, to `2029` | growth of **`USD 170.8B`** | Technavio, via FutureCIO |

🔵 **The spread is the finding.** 🔴 **A `2026` figure of `$6.4B`, `$10.6B` or `$5.37B` depending on
the publisher is not a market size, it is three different definitions** — and a proposal that
quotes one without the definition is quoting a press release.

## Opportunities by region

### North America

🔴 **Demand, as cited.** Stanford's `2026` AI Index puts **`50`–`84%` of K-12 students** and
**~`90%` of US higher-education students** using AI for schoolwork. The PIE Network tracked
**~`100` state bills in `2026`** touching student AI use; Ohio was first to require AI policies in
every K-12 district; Illinois (`S.B. 1920`) requires state guidance by **`2026-07-01`**; Tennessee
requires every public institution, LEA and charter to adopt a policy. Federal action remains
executive — a December `2025` order for a light-touch national framework and a K-12 AI task force
(ECS). Google has pledged **`$1B`** to higher education; TED, Khan Academy and ETS have launched an
AI institute for higher ed.

🟢 **Supply, as measured today.** The region's open supply is **institutional**: MIT Open Learning
publishes a tutor library, a chatbot backend and an internal agent toolkit; Learning Commons and
Anthropic co-publish rubrics, skills and an evaluation harness. 🔴 **The most-wanted integration
surface — an MCP server over Canvas LMS (`104★`) — is UNGRANTED** (`0` licence files in the tree).

🟢 **The opening.** 🔵 **Policy is the budget line**: `~100` bills and a state-by-state patchwork
mean districts and systems must produce POLICY-CONFORMANT deployments on deadlines, and the
rubric/evaluation layer that proves conformance is Apache-2.0 and MIT today. 🟢 **Build the
evidence layer first** (`learning-commons-org/evaluators` + `anthropics/k12-teacher-skills`), 🔴
**and treat `knowledge-graph`'s Gated datasets as out of scope for anything shipped** →
`agents/top.md`, `P92`.

### EMEA

🔴 **Demand and regulation, as cited.** The EU AI Act is the frame: emotion-recognition prohibition
applied **`2025-02-02`**, transparency obligations **`2026-08-02`**, and the high-risk education
obligations **`2027-12-02`** (one source records this as a slip from an original August `2026`
date; the Commission's Article 6 guidelines went to consultation in May `2026`). The UK set
generative-AI safety standards for schools in **January `2026`**; the UAE issued **`25`** generative
AI restrictions for schools in **February `2026`**. Eurydice's *Digital education at school in
Europe 2026* reports uneven access to advanced AI at school level; one EU analysis cites **`13%`**
of pupils in Greece attending a school with Wi-Fi against **`100%`** in the Netherlands. An Epson
Europe survey of `3,360` respondents reports **`80%`** of educators concerned about the pace of AI
in the classroom, and a six-country adult-education study reports **>`90%`** of Spanish respondents
using AI in their work, largely without a regulatory frame. 🔴 **A UK policy paper reports
school-technology investment down `96%` since `2020`, with `0.7%` of edtech investment going to AI
tools.**

🟢 **Supply, as measured today.** 🟢 **EMEA is the only region where a university publishes BOTH an
open teaching platform and its open AI layer**: `ls1intum/Artemis` (MIT) with `edutelligence`
(MIT, `athena` + `iris`). France's national MOOC operator publishes `richie` (MIT) — 🔴 **and
`p858` had recorded France as absent from GitHub entirely** (`T211`).

🟢 **The opening.** 🔵 **Two deadlines and one architecture.** The `2026-08-02` transparency
obligations and the `2027-12-02` high-risk obligations give a named date to sell against, and
Annex III point `3` makes "assessing learning outcomes" high-risk by construction. 🟢 **The build
that satisfies both is already open and already European**: Artemis + EduTelligence, self-hosted,
with the agent PROPOSING and the human RECORDING. 🔴 **Do not build it on
[`ls1intum/AngelosAI`](https://github.com/ls1intum/AngelosAI) — its grant is `MIT with Commons
Clause` and names no holder** (`T207`) → `P92`.

### APAC

🔴 **Demand, as cited.** The Philippines' Department of Education launched **AGAP.AI** in January
`2026`, a nationwide responsible-AI programme aimed at `1.5M` people, with a National Education Plan
`2026`–`2035` embedding AI and digital literacy at every level. Australia's education ministers
backed a national review of the Generative AI Framework for Schools in June `2025`. An EdTech Hub
briefing reports **~`86%` of Southeast Asian teachers already using AI tools**; Ipsos's `2026`
Education Monitor finds weaker support for banning AI in Asian schools than elsewhere. Market
sources cite a **`20.9%` CAGR** for APAC AI-in-education (`2024`–`2029`), **>`3,000`** regional
edtech players, and APAC leading global edtech investment in `2023` at ~**`$12B`**.

🔴 **Supply, as measured today: `0` rows placed in APAC, and the reason is this base's instrument.**
Two substantial Chinese-language projects were shelved `Global` rather than APAC
(`Li-Evan/BloomTutor`, `312★`, MIT; `flysheep-ai/education-skills`, `125★`, MIT), because language
is not region (`T935`) and the account-origin endpoint is `403` here. 🔵 **`p859`'s APAC finding
still stands and is unchallenged: India's Sunbird estate is MIT, `393`-forked, and has NO AI layer.**

🟢 **The opening.** 🔵 **APAC is the region where the mandate is national and the AI layer is
absent** — the Philippines' plan and India's platform are both state-scale with no incumbent agent
to displace. 🔴 **The gating risk is equally clear: Sunbird's flagship repository's last human
commit is `2023-04-26`, so the liveness check runs BEFORE the proposal, not after** (`P91`).

### LATAM

🔴 **Demand, as cited.** A Digital Education Council survey (February `2026`) reports **`92%` of
Latin American students and `79%` of faculty** engaging with AI, while **only `30%` of universities
have published AI-use policies**. UNESCO's September `2026` report on AI in higher education in the
region describes adoption as *"neither uniform nor evenly distributed"*. An observatory launched in
April `2026` with CAF, CENIA, the OECD and Harvard scholars addresses a learning gap in which **six
in ten sixth-graders** fall below minimum proficiency in reading and mathematics; a UNESCO–CENIA
agreement (March `2026`) pairs ethical AI standards with **Latam-GPT**. The IDB has catalogued
**`193`** classroom AI initiatives and argues the region is building hybrid, context-sensitive
systems rather than importing them. EdTech Hub's AI Observatory has partnered with **Fundación
Ceibal** 🇺🇾 (September `2026`). 🔴 **No regional AI-in-education law was found in these legs.**

🟢 **Supply, as measured today — and it is better than this base has previously recorded.**
🟢 **Brazil's national curriculum is available as MIT-licensed, provenance-tracked open data**
(`bncc-dev/bncc-dados`: `1,721` learning objectives in JSON, SQLite and CSV), and a Colombian
university has published an MIT autonomous tutor for RURAL higher education
(`LabSirius/TutorIA`). 🔴 **Both are low-star rows that no ranked search returns** — `26★` and
`0★`.

🟢 **The opening.** 🔵 **`92%` student adoption against `30%` institutional policy is the widest
demand/governance gap of the four regions**, and it is an advisory-and-policy engagement before it
is a build. 🟢 **The build that follows has a licence trail for the first time**: BNCC curriculum
data (MIT) + an MIT tutor + Apache-2.0 teacher skills, with the Open edX estate kept at arm's
length through the Apache-2.0 `XBlock` contract rather than forked → `P93`.

### Global — what belongs to no region, and the two gaps this pass leaves open

## Opportunities by region

🔴 **Every market and adoption figure in this section is `CITED, NOT READ` from WebSearch
summaries on `2026-11-16`, and several are secondary aggregators that disagree with each other —
the ranges below are reported as ranges for that reason.** 🟢 **Every SUPPLY figure is byte-read
or enumerated by this pass.**

### Global

🟡 **Market size, three estimates that do not agree:** ~**`$10.6B`** in `2026` → **`$42.5B`** by
`2030` at `41.5%` CAGR (Research and Markets); ~**`$10.4B`** → **`$32.27B`** by `2030` at `31.2%`
(Resourcera); ~**`$12.3B`** for `2026` (HolonIQ-derived). 🔵 **Treat the direction as solid and the
magnitude as ±20%.**

🔴 **The gap that is the actual opportunity, and it is a governance gap rather than a technology
gap.** 🟢 Student use is effectively saturated — reported at **`92%`** of students using AI in
`2025` against `66%` in `2024`, with another source at `86%` and `24%` daily. 🔴 **UNESCO: only
`10%` of schools and universities have formal AI guidelines, and only ~`25%` of educators report
adequate training.** 🔵 **Institution-wide adoption in higher education moved `49%` → `66%` in one
year (Ellucian).** 🟢 **So the buyer's unmet need is policy, provenance and audit — not another
chatbot**, which is the same conclusion the OECD's `2026` Digital Education Outlook reaches when
it argues for purpose-built educational AI over wrapped general-purpose tools (`T98`).

🟢 **Globant-shaped offer:** the `P91` liveness-and-grant audit productised as a
**procurement-grade open-source due-diligence report**. 🔵 **`T204` shows the entire sector grades
freshness from a field that a bot can forge; every institution inheriting an open-source stack has
that defect, and a licence claim nobody can reproduce from the file it names is a procurement
finding.** 🔵 Sells into all five regions and needs no local presence.

### North America

🟢 **The best-supplied region in this pass and the only non-European one with a readable public AI
layer.** Ed-Fi is **Apache-2.0 across `28` of `31` repositories**, genuinely live (`5` of `5`
within `6` days of HEAD), and ships both a production RAG agent (`Fiona`) and a `13`-skill agent
library.

🟡 **Regulation is STATE-led and it is a tailwind, not a barrier** (`CITED, NOT READ`): ~`134`
AI-in-education bills across `31` states in the `2026` session, weighted to student-data privacy
and classroom-use limits. 🟢 North Carolina (Session Law `2026-41`) sets AI-literacy standards with
a **state model policy due `2026-12-31`** and teacher training by **`2028-06-30`**; Idaho approved
a statewide generative-AI framework in `2026-08`; Maryland, Idaho, Oklahoma and Virginia require
districts to adopt AI policies; New York's `A 9190` would restrict classroom AI below grade `9`.
🟢 Federally, the US Department of Education issued a responsible-use framework (`2026-03`) and
K-12 guidance (`2026-04`). 🔵 **A mandated district policy with a date on it is a funded
engagement.**

🟢 **Offer:** `P90` — the Ed-Fi-native AI layer, sold to districts and state agencies against the
model-policy deadlines above, with `P87`'s early-warning build attached and its conformance
argument re-pointed from Annex III to state statute. 🔵 **Differentiator: a studio that can read
`Ed-Fi-Data-Standard v6.2.0` and `Fiona` speaks the buyer's data model on day one.**

### EMEA

🟢 **Carried from `p858`, still the strongest region for public-sector supply** — `305`
repositories, seven agencies, and the only national OER AI fleet in this base (`openeduhub`,
Apache-2.0). 🔵 **Licence geography decides the commercial shape: Finland EUPL (co-build), Norway
and the Netherlands MIT/Apache (fork, close, ship), Germany Apache-2.0 apps with CC0 vocabularies.**

🟡 **The regulatory calendar moved, and the movement is the selling point** (`CITED, NOT READ`):
**Regulation (EU) `2026/1744`, the Digital Omnibus on AI, pushed the Annex III high-risk duties for
education — admissions, grading, learning-outcome assessment and exam proctoring — from
`2026-08-02` out to `2027-12-02`.** 🟢 **Article 50 transparency obligations DID start on
`2026-08-02`** (a classroom chatbot must disclose it is AI; generated content must be
machine-detectable), the Article 4 AI-literacy duty is live, the education emotion-recognition
prohibition has applied since `2025-02`, and further prohibitions plus a labelling deadline land
`2026-12-02`.

🔵 **Read the delay correctly: it is `12` months of runway to build conformance IN, not permission
to skip it.** 🟢 **Offer:** `P88`'s licence-boundary architecture plus an Annex III conformance
package timed to `2027-12-02`, with sovereign inference already a config value (`GWDG`,
`AcademicCloud / KISSKI`) rather than a rewrite.

### APAC

🟢 **The largest permissive supply in this pass and the largest vacuum.** India's Sunbird is
**MIT across `162` of `182` repositories**, forked `393` / `318` / `128` times — a deployment
population, not an audience (`T202`) — and 🔴 **ships no AI layer at all.** 🔴 **Its flagship
`sunbird-devops` last had a human commit on `2023-04-26` while the API reports `2026-03-25`
(`T204`).**

🟡 **Regulation diverges sharply by jurisdiction** (`CITED, NOT READ`): 🔴 **China is the most
binding** — public-facing services need security assessment and algorithm filing before launch, and
the anthropomorphic-AI-services measures issued `2026-04-10` took effect `2026-07-15`, which bears
directly on a companion-style tutor. 🟡 **India** amended its IT Rules effective `2026-02-20` and
signalled in `2026-07` that it may pursue dedicated AI legislation. 🟢 **Japan's AI Promotion Act
stays light-touch**, with a second AI Basic Plan approved `2026-07-14`. 🔴 **Vietnam's Decision
`33/2026/QD-TTg` names education — including automated assessment — among six high-risk sectors.**
🔵 Singapore is reported as the regional adoption leader.

🟢 **Offer, and it is the clearest build opportunity in this pass:** an MIT-licensed AI layer over
Sunbird for a state or national deployment — content tagging, differentiated path generation and a
teacher-facing assistant — built BESIDE the platform over its APIs. 🔵 **MIT means the studio may
ship it closed.** 🔴 **Scope the staleness honestly: price a fork-and-maintain of `sunbird-devops`
into the first phase, because `393` downstream deployments inherit it unmaintained.**
🔵 **`P91` is the instrument that makes that conversation survivable.**

### LATAM

🔴 **Demand is the highest in this base and adoptable supply is the lowest. That inversion IS the
market.** 🟡 Demand (`CITED, NOT READ`): a Digital Education Council survey of `30,000+` higher-ed

## Opportunities by region

### North America

🟡 **`CITED, NOT READ`.** 🟢 **The federal layer acquired a shape this pass**: the Education
Commission of the States reports that **executive orders issued between `2025` and early `2026`

instruction** — 🔵 a posture, not a binding rule, which keeps the `35+` states with their own

  **`41.7%` of global growth**. 🔴 **Two houses, two definitions — quote the segment, never the

- 🟡 **Privacy is the second front and it is not an education regulator**: the **NTIA** has said

## 🟢 Sixty-second pass (`p857`, 2026-11-16) — **the academic-risk tier placed by region, and the regional story is a GOVERNANCE gap, not a technology gap**

🔵 **The one regional claim this pass can make that it could not make before.** `p856` found a
region whose open supply is excellent and whose law restricts the obvious use of it. 🟢 **`p857`
finds the opposite shape: a tier where the technology is uniformly available, uniformly cheap and
uniformly unmaintained — so the differentiator between regions is not what they can BUILD but
whether they are ALLOWED to deploy it and whether anyone is governing it.**

| | |
|---|---|
| 🟢 **Supply is region-neutral here** | The `9` rows filed today are MIT and the corpus is CC BY 4.0. 🔵 **No region has a supply advantage in academic-risk prediction** — unlike the discourse tier, where EMEA supplied the accuracy leaders |
| 🔴 **Demand is NOT** | This is the AI Act's own high-risk category. 🔵 The same build is a procurement asset in EMEA, a state-by-state patchwork in NA, a jurisdiction-by-jurisdiction problem in APAC, and an ungoverned free-for-all in LATAM |
| 🔴 **The corpus is EMEA and everyone trains on it** | OULAD is UK, `2013–2014`, `32,593` adult distance learners. 🔴 **Every region deploying this tier is deploying a model validated on a British population from twelve years ago** → `857-G1` |
| 🟢 **The thing to sell is GOVERNANCE, not the model** | UNESCO IESALC `2026`: `87%` of LATAM HEIs use AI, `26%` have a strategy, `55%` have no AI budget. 🔵 **The model is a weekend; the defensible deployment is the engagement** |

🔴 **Every market, adoption and regulatory figure below is `CITED, NOT READ` from WebSearch
summaries and says so.** 🟢 Every licence, byte count and `sha256₁₂` on these shelves is byte-read
`2026-11-16`.

## Opportunities by region — as filed by `p857` (historical, superseded above)

### North America

🟡 **`CITED, NOT READ`.** The 2026 session produced AI-in-education bills at a scale the trackers
disagree about — one reports **`134` bills across `31` states**, another **`~71` across `27`**.
🟢 **The disagreement does not change the shape: `35+` states now have official AI guidance from
their education departments**, and the posture is research-transparency-guardrails rather than
mandated adoption.

- 🟡 **New York (`NY A 9190`)** restricts classroom AI use to **grade 9 and above**, with exceptions
  for diagnostics and special education. 🔴 **A K-8 early-warning deployment in New York is a
  different product from the same build in a neighbouring state.**
- 🟡 **North Carolina (`Session Law 2026-41`)** sets AI-literacy standards, a **state model policy
  by `2026-12-31`**, local school-unit policies by **`2027-06-30`**, and teacher training by
  **June 2028**.
- 🟡 The federal **Department of Education finalised its AI grant-priority rule on `2026-04-13`**.
- 🟡 An **April 2026 ADA deadline** for large public institutions is pushing accessibility tooling.

🟢 **The opportunity.** 🔵 **Sell the policy artefact, not the predictor.** Districts have a dated
obligation to produce a policy and train staff, and no capacity to write either. 🟢
[`mitodl/ol-data-platform`](https://github.com/mitodl/ol-data-platform) (BSD-3, pushed `2026-11-16`)
is the credible NA reference for the pipeline underneath, and
[`Western-OC2-Lab/…-eLearning-datasets`](https://github.com/Western-OC2-Lab/Student-Performance-and-Engagement-Prediction-eLearning-datasets)
(MIT) is the **only non-OULAD corpus** with which to show a model works on a non-UK population.

### EMEA

🟡 **`CITED, NOT READ`, and the date moved.** The AI Act makes **admissions, evaluation of learning
outcomes and exam scoring HIGH-RISK**. 🟡 **The Digital Omnibus (`Regulation (EU) 2026/1744`) moved
the education high-risk obligations to `2027-12-02`** — 🔵 guides still citing **August 2026**
predate the omnibus and are stale. 🟢 **Already in force since February 2025: the AI-literacy duty,
and the BAN on emotion recognition in education.** 🟡 Chatbot transparency applies **August 2026**.

- 🟡 The **European Commission and OECD published an AI-literacy framework for primary and secondary
  education on `2026-06-18`**; the Commission updated its ethical guidelines on AI and data in
  teaching the same month.
- 🟡 The **Council of the EU** names the **European Digital Education Hub** and the **European School
  Education Platform** as delivery channels, with digital sovereignty attached.
- 🟡 Schools are **deployers**, not providers — a narrower duty, but it does not disappear.

🟢 **The opportunity, and it is the largest in this tier.** 🔴 **A high-risk date in December 2027 is
an eighteen-month procurement runway that has already started.** 🔵 The deliverable is a conformance
package — risk management, data governance, logging, human oversight, technical documentation —
wrapped around a model that is itself free. 🟢 **EMEA also supplies the two rows that make this
defensible:** [`MaxvanHaastrecht/Federated-Learning-Analytics`](https://github.com/MaxvanHaastrecht/Federated-Learning-Analytics)
(MIT, trains across institutions **without centralising student records** — the data-minimisation
story told in code) and [`KuznetsovKarazin/oulad-early-warning`](https://github.com/KuznetsovKarazin/oulad-early-warning)
(MIT, interpretable, budgets instructor workload — **human oversight that a teacher can actually
exercise**). 🔴 **Do not deliver this as a Moodle plugin**: `moodle-local_lytix` and `ANALYSE` show
the layer is GPL/AGPL inside the LMS.

### APAC

🟡 **`CITED, NOT READ`. The region does not have one answer and a single build will not clear it.**

- 🟢 **South Korea's AI Basic Act took effect `2026-01-22`** — described as the region's first

  and South Korea** and **fell in Indonesia**; 🔴 **Australia and New Zealand are the outliers with
  the highest support for a ban.**

🟢 **The opportunity.** 🔵 **Sell the same model with a swappable compliance shell** — Vietnam's
high-risk classification and Korea's statute demand documentation the Japanese and Australian
deployments do not. 🔴 **The `18`-month Vietnamese transition is a dated, named window.** 🟢 Prefer

## 🟢 Sixty-first pass (`p856`, 2026-11-16) — **the classroom-DISCOURSE tier placed by region, and the region that SUPPLIES it best is the region that most restricts it**

🔵 **The one regional claim this pass can make that it could not make before.** `p855` separated a
region with NO open supply from a region with UNUSABLE open supply. 🟢 **`p856` adds a third case
that neither covers: a region whose open supply is EXCELLENT and whose LAW makes the obvious
application of it illegal.** 🔴 **EMEA publishes the two strongest permissive rows in this tier and
is the jurisdiction where pointing them at a classroom is most constrained.**

| | |
|---|---|
| 🟢 **EMEA supplies this tier** | [`juanmc2005/diart`](https://github.com/juanmc2005/diart) (MIT, `1,105 B`, Université Paris-Saclay + CNRS) and [`BUTSpeechFIT/DiariZen`](https://github.com/BUTSpeechFIT/DiariZen) (MIT code, Brno Univ. of Technology) are the accuracy and latency leaders here — 🔴 **and `DiariZen`'s weights are `CC BY-NC 4.0`** |
| 🔴 **EMEA also restricts it hardest** | Emotion recognition in education is PROHIBITED under the EU AI Act, and voice is biometric data. 🟢 **The build that is legal is talk-TIME and talk-ROLE; the build that is not is inferring how a child FEELS** |
| 🟢 **The substrate is region-neutral, the classroom layer is not** | `pyAudioAnalysis` (Apache-2.0) needs no weights and no network, so the tier's floor is available in every region including under data-residency rules |
| 🔴 **LATAM's gap is in the CLASSROOM layer, not the AUDIO layer** | ES+PT legs return meeting and court-hearing transcribers and `0` granted classroom projects → `856-G5` |

## Opportunities by region — as filed by `p856` (historical, superseded above)

🟢 **The commercial trigger is a DEADLINE, and it is `2027-06-30`.** `CITED, NOT READ`: North

🔴 **The blocker specific to THIS tier, and it is not AI law.** Classroom audio capture runs into
state wiretap and two-party-consent statutes before it reaches any AI statute. 🟢 **So the
North-American engagement leads with consent architecture and retention, and
[`DMlabcodeorg/WhoSaidWhat`](https://github.com/DMlabcodeorg/WhoSaidWhat) (MIT, ICDL 2024,
validated against human expert coding) is the defensibility asset** — a talk-ratio number that a
school board challenges must cite a method someone validated.

is 🔴 **a CODE-ONLY grant** and was last pushed `2025-04-22`.

### EMEA

🟢 **Strongest permissive supply in this tier of any region, and the tightest constraint on its use.**
`CITED, NOT READ`: the EU AI Act treats admissions, student evaluation and exam scoring as
high-risk; the Article 4 AI-literacy duty has applied since `2025-02-02`; emotion recognition in
education has been prohibited since February 2025; the `2026` Digital Omnibus moved stand-alone

A late-2026 Council of the EU document stresses digital sovereignty and reducing dependence on
non-European AI tools in education.

that reports TALK TIME and TURN STRUCTURE and refuses to infer emotion is 🟢 **sellable as AI Act
conformance**, and its substrate is European (`diart`: Paris-Saclay + CNRS; `DiariZen`: Brno).
🔵 **Sovereignty-conscious buyers can be shown a pipeline with no US dependency and, via
`pyAudioAnalysis`, no model weights at all.** 🔴 **The works-council/union review is the real gate:
teacher audio is employee monitoring, and the build survives only if the teacher owns the output.**

| | |
|---|---|
| 🔴 **LATAM's essay/exam estate is large, current, and carries no terms at all** | `18` addresses byte-probed at `31` filenames × resolved default ref: 🔴 **`16` `NO-GRANT-AT-ROOT`, `1` dead, `1` proprietary-in-Spanish, `0` permissive.** 🔵 The ENEM essay is a national examination marked on five published competencies; the open estate around it is a dozen active projects and a studio can use none of them → `855-G3`, `T188` |
| 🟢 **EMEA publishes the same category under copyleft — which is a RECOVERABLE position** | [`UFV-INGINF/JorGPT`](https://github.com/UFV-INGINF/JorGPT), a Spanish engineering school's own exam-marking tool, GPL-3.0 byte-read `35,149 B`. 🔵 **Copyleft is a negotiation; no licence is a dead end.** The same category, two regions, two different failure modes |
| 🟢 **APAC's permissive supply is real but SEEDED, not stocked** | `3` of this pass's `9` agent rows are APAC-published and permissive — but at `1★`, `2★` and `0★`. 🔴 The region's traction sits on copyleft (`MixTeX` `1,636★` AGPL-3.0, `CogniLoop` `498★` AGPL-3.0) and on unlicensed coursework |
| 🟢 **The document-perception tier is the most EVENLY distributed capability this base has filed** | EMEA `eynollah` (Berlin State Library), `OMR-Datasets`, `tf-end-to-end`, `Orchestra` (Ain Shams), `HTG_evaluation` · APAC `oar-ocr`, `JiaJiaOCR`, `LaTeX_OCR`, `indic-htr`, `Polyphonic-TrOMR`, `web-omr` · North America `moonlight` (Google) · Global `layout-parser`, `TexTeller`. 🔵 **No region holds this tier, so no region is behind in it** |

### 🔴 The regional-claim audit `854-G1` prescribed, priced

🟢 **`854-G1` ended: *"every 'no supply in region X' claim older than this pass is UNVERIFIED on
this axis. The number to recover first is how many such claims exist."* It was recovered.**

| Measurement over the `11` live pages | Count |
|---|---|
| absence sentences (broad tier) | **`2,270`** |
| of which carry a REGION or LANGUAGE token | 🔴 **`333`** (`14.7%`) |
| SUPPLY absence claims (the class that sends a client to BUILD) | 🔴 **`855`** |
| of those, UNDATED | 🔴 **`598`** (`69.9%`) |

🔵 **`333` regional absence claims is the re-probe bill, and `p855`'s own result says the re-probe
must be selective rather than mechanical:** 🔴 **a blanket in-language re-probe at the wrong
altitude returns `92%` new addresses and `1%` usable ones**, so re-probing all `333` by presence
alone would manufacture false withdrawals faster than it corrected real ones. 🟢 **Re-probe the
claims whose noun is an ARTEFACT; leave the workflow-noun claims standing.** → `855-G1`, `855-G5`.

🔴 **And the instrument that produced those numbers was itself defective.**
[`ingest/absence-claims.py`](../ingest/absence-claims.py) resolves its root to
`os.path.dirname(__file__)` — the `ingest/` directory — unless `KB_ROOT` is set. 🔴 **Run the way
its own docstring shows, it reports `0 pages, 0 shelf rows, 0 distinct addresses` and `0 absence
claims`: a clean bill of health from an instrument that read nothing.** 🟢 **With `KB_ROOT` set it
reads `11` pages, `17,354` shelf rows and `6,068` distinct addresses.** 🔵 **This is
`p355-cwd-portability`'s class landing on the one instrument `568-A` built to audit absence
claims.** → `855-G5`.

## Opportunities by region — as filed by `p855` (historical, superseded above)

### North America

🟢 **The perception tier's only North-American row is a corporate orphan, and that is the
opportunity.** [`tensorflow/moonlight`](https://github.com/tensorflow/moonlight) — Apache-2.0,
`330★`, Google-published, 🔴 **pushed `2022-06-02` and unmaintained under a first-party org, which
means no one will merge a fix.** 🔵 A permissive, maintained OMR fork has a clear owner-shaped hole.

🟢 **Policy context, `CITED, NOT READ`:** ~`134` AI-in-education bills across `31` states; at least
`15` states have introduced or passed legislation addressing AI in education specifically; North
Carolina `Session Law 2026-41` sets a model policy due `2026-12-31` and district policies by
`2027-06-30`; the U.S. Department of Education released `$169M` in FIPSE awards including
responsible-AI provisions. 🔵 **The district-policy deadline is the commercial trigger: `2027-06-30`
is inside a normal procurement cycle from now.**

🔴 **The sector signal that should temper any pitch:** only `45%` of the most-used edtech tools have
published research meeting an ESSA evidence tier, and the market is reported as rising revenue
against falling investment. 🟢 **A studio that can produce an evidence package alongside the build
is selling the scarce half.**

### EMEA

🟢 **This region is the best-supplied in the document-perception tier and it is not close.**
[`qurator-spk/eynollah`](https://github.com/qurator-spk/eynollah) (Apache-2.0, `418★`, Berlin State
Library, pushed `2026-11-13`) is the ACTIVE layout analyser; `OMR-Datasets`, `tf-end-to-end`
(`OMR-Research`), `Orchestra` and `Mozart` (both Ain Shams, Egypt) and `HTG_evaluation` are all
permissive and EMEA-authored. 🔵 **Two independent permissive OMR pipelines out of Egypt is a MEA
capability worth naming in a region this base has repeatedly had to declare empty.**

🟡 **The region's marking tools are copyleft rather than absent** —
[`UFV-INGINF/JorGPT`](https://github.com/UFV-INGINF/JorGPT), GPL-3.0, published by a Spanish
university marking its own exams. 🔵 **That is a negotiable position: a GPL tool can be deployed to
the institution that owns it, and the institution is the client.**

🟢 **Regulatory context, `CITED, NOT READ`:** the EU AI Act is the binding frame, with high-risk
obligations covering education reported as applying from **`2027-12-02`** (a date still contested
across sources — 🔴 **treat as CITED and verify against the Commission's own pages before it is
priced into a proposal**); a Commission consultation on high-risk classification guidelines closed
`2026-06-23`; UK DfE genAI product-safety standards (Jan 2026) and Ofqual coursework-integrity rules
(Mar 2026); Eurydice's *Digital education at school in Europe 2026* (`2026-09-22`). Futuresource
reports `67%` of teachers now using AI tools and a shift from pilots to structured implementation.

### APAC

🟢 **The clearest single opportunity this pass found anywhere, and it is a script problem.**
[`Sanny26/indic-htr`](https://github.com/Sanny26/indic-htr) — MIT, 🔴 **`8★` / `4` forks** — is the
only permissive multi-Indic-script handwriting artefact this base holds. 🔵 **India examines school
students in a dozen writing systems; every other row in the handwriting layer assumes Latin script.
This is a seed, not a supply, and the gap is `855-G5`.**

🟡 **Japan's national device estate is addressable through an artefact published the day before this
pass:** [`GIGAyama/Online-Publisher-pro`](https://github.com/GIGAyama/Online-Publisher-pro), MIT,
`0★`, built for the **GIGA School** one-device-per-child programme. 🔵 **`T116`'s rule — find what
the state has already deployed before scoping an agent — applied to a device estate.**

🔴 **APAC's traction is copyleft:** `RQLuo/MixTeX-Latex-OCR` (`1,636★`, AGPL-3.0),
`itsmorninghao/CogniLoop` (`498★`, AGPL-3.0, the most complete marking loop found anywhere this
pass). 🟢 **Permissive APAC rows exist — `oar-ocr`, `JiaJiaOCR`, `LaTeX_OCR`, `Polyphonic-TrOMR`,
`web-omr` — but the thing a client will have already found is the AGPL one.**

🟢 **Regulatory context, `CITED, NOT READ`:** Korea's **AI Basic Act in force `2026-01-22`**
(education high-impact) and Vietnam's AI law **in force `2026-03-01`** (education high-risk, with
pre-deployment registration) are the two BINDING instruments; the UAE Ministry of Education issued
`25` generative-AI prohibitions for schools (Feb 2026); Australia's TEQSA requires institutional
genAI action plans. Ipsos's Education Monitor 2026 reports lower support for classroom AI bans
across the Asian markets surveyed.

### LATAM

🔴 **The region's finding this pass is not scarcity. It is an active estate with no terms on it.**
`18` addresses in the ENEM/exam-correction category, byte-probed at `31` filenames each:
🔴 **`16` with no licence file at all, `1` dead address served live by the search index, `1` carrying
a bespoke `LICENCIA PROPIETARIA — ZARCONT`, and `0` permissive.**

🔵 **The engagement consequence is specific and it is the opposite of the usual advice:** there is no
adoption path here, so the work is a BUILD — but it is a *well-specified* build, because a dozen
independent projects have already converged on the same five-competency ENEM rubric and that
convergence is free design input. 🟢 **Costed as `P85` on
[`compose/patterns.md`](../compose/patterns.md): `6`–`8` weeks on a permissive substrate.**

🔵 **This is `854-G7` — Spanish pronunciation supply with no grants — confirmed in Portuguese and in
a different tier, which promotes it from an observation to a regional structural property.**
🟢 **The actionable rule for any LATAM engagement: assume the reference implementations exist, assume
you may not use them, and budget to rebuild rather than to integrate.**

🟢 **Adoption context, `CITED, NOT READ`:** Digital Education Council LATAM survey 2026 (`29`
institutions) — `92%` of students and `79%` of faculty engaging with AI, `67%` of students weekly,
🔴 **but only `30%` believe their institution has responded effectively**; UNESCO IESALC working
paper (Sep 2026) reports adoption *"neither uniform nor evenly distributed"* with only ~`30%` of
universities publishing AI-use policies; IDB estimates AI could add **`5.1%`** to regional GDP over a
decade; Chile's CENIA–UNESCO agreement and Latam-GPT; EdTech Hub and Fundación Ceibal partnered
Sep 2026. 🔴 **No concrete national AI-in-education regulation surfaced on this pass's LATAM leg —
recorded as a GAP in the evidence, not as an absence of regulation.**

### Global

🟢 **The document-perception tier is the most region-neutral capability this base has ever filed,
and that is itself the opportunity.** [`Layout-Parser/layout-parser`](https://github.com/Layout-Parser/layout-parser)
(Apache-2.0, `5,779★`) and [`OleehyO/TexTeller`](https://github.com/OleehyO/TexTeller)
(Apache-2.0, `764★`) are not attached to any jurisdiction, any curriculum or any language of
instruction. 🔵 **A perception spine built once is re-pointed at a new region by swapping the
handwriting model and the rubric — stages `2b` and `4` of `P85` — and nothing else.**

🔴 **The global constraint that applies everywhere and is easy to miss:** `6` of this tier's
traction leaders are copyleft and they OUTRANK the permissive ones in search (`T187`). 🟢 **Any
engagement in any region needs the exclusion list — `texify`, `LaTeX_OCR_PRO`, `MixTeX`, `Texo`,
`LaTeXSnipper`, `homr` — written into the architecture decision record before a developer reaches
for the top search hit.**

🔴 **And the global evidence gap, stated rather than left silent:** this pass found `0` open
artefacts that measure AGREEMENT between a machine mark and a human marker on a client's own
scripts. 🟢 **`P81` is the recipe that ships an accuracy number and it remains the scarce half of
every marking proposal** — the supply side of this tier is now well stocked and the validation side
is empty.

## Opportunities by region — as filed by `p854` (historical, superseded above)

### North America

🟢 **The policy shape prices the work: ~`134` AI-in-education bills across `31` states in `2026`,
and the pattern is literacy, transparency and guardrails rather than adoption mandates.**
🔵 **North Carolina's `Session Law 2026-41` is the most procurement-relevant single instrument
found — AI-literacy standards, a state model policy due `2026-12-31`, local district policies due
`2027-06-30`** — 🟢 **which is a dated, funded compliance deadline with a district-by-district
delivery shape, and that is a studio engagement.**

🟡 **The opportunity is TEACHER-FACING and EVIDENCE-FACING, not student-facing automation.**
🔴 **California's `AB 1159` would bar student data from training models and New York's `A 9190`
would restrict classroom AI below grade `9`** — both push value toward systems that demonstrably
do not retain or train on student data. 🟢 **Which is the same architecture `T182` names: scoring
that happens locally and ships a grade.** 🔵 **Pair `krayin/laravel-crm` (MIT) on the admissions
side with an on-device assessment path, and the data-residency story is the differentiator rather
than the model.**

### EMEA

🔴 **The region's measured supply gap is the sharpest on this page and it is MEA, not Europe.**
🟢 **Arabic recitation and tajweed learning is a mass-market category with institutional demand:
`54` repositories answer `تلاوة`, with a `11★`/`8`-fork flagship.** 🔴 **Not one of them scores a
recitation, and the flagship ships no licence** (`854-G3`). 🟢 **So the EMEA opportunity is
unusually well defined: the demand artefacts exist and can be studied, the scoring layer does not
exist in any language, and the only permissive Arabic row this base holds is a playback app.**

🔵 **Europe's half is a PROCUREMENT-GATE opportunity rather than a supply gap.** 🟢 **The concrete,
dated gates found this pass are the UK DfE's generative-AI product safety standards (January
`2026`) and Ofqual's coursework-integrity resources (March `2026`)** — 🔵 **a product that arrives
already mapped to those documents wins on evaluation time.** 🔴 **The EU AI Act's education
high-risk date remains contested across sources (`2026-08-02` enforcement machinery vs a reported
slip of stand-alone high-risk duties to `2027-12-02`); this base does not resolve it and no bid
should assume the later date.** 🔵 **The UAE's KHDA guidance to Dubai private schools is the
clearest Gulf entry point.**

### APAC

🔴 **Stop treating APAC as one posture: it now contains two BINDING AI laws in force this year and
two explicitly voluntary regimes.** 🟢 **Korea's `AI Basic Act` (in force `2026-01-22`) names
education high-impact, with `2026` reported as a guidance-focused grace period — a `12`-month
window to arrive compliant before enforcement bites.** 🟢 **Vietnam's AI law (in force
`2026-03-01`) puts education in its high-risk class and requires PRE-DEPLOYMENT REGISTRATION in a
National AI Database** — 🔵 **a registration step is a delivery milestone and must be in the plan,
not discovered at go-live.** 🟡 **Japan and Singapore remain voluntary; Australia's TEQSA requires
every higher-education provider to file an institutional action plan on generative AI, which is a
document-production engagement in itself.**

🟢 **The supply news is good and it is new: this region's permissive on-device assessment supply
exists in Japanese and Korean** — `nanteiu-pronunciation-model` (Apache-2.0), `yomori`
(Apache-2.0), `PronunciationEvaluator` (MIT) — 🔴 **all at `0`–`6★`, so they are starting points,
not dependencies.** 🔵 **Korea's combination of a high-impact designation and a permissive local
scorer is the single best-matched opportunity on this page.**

### LATAM

🟢 **The opportunity is the subtraction: `87%` of surveyed institutions use AI in at least one
area, `26%` have a formal AI strategy, `55%` have no AI budget** (UNESCO IESALC `2026`, `200`
institutions across `19` countries). 🔵 **`92%` of students and `79%` of faculty are already using
AI (Digital Education Council LATAM `2026`), and in Brazil `53%` of school administrators use it
while `22%` have any guidelines (Cetic.br via UNESCO).** 🔴 **The demand is not the thing to sell.
The GOVERNANCE is: strategy, guidelines, data-rights schedules and evaluation — work that `55%` of
these institutions have no budget line for, which is a procurement problem to solve as part of the
sale.** 🟢 **A Universidad de los Andes count of `716` AI regulatory instruments across `23`
countries means fragmentation, so a multi-country rollout needs a per-jurisdiction matrix rather
than one regional policy.** 🟢 **The IDB's November `2026` estimate — AI could add `5.1%` to
regional GDP over a decade — is the macro argument for the funding conversation.**

🟡 **On supply, LATAM is now better placed than `p853` believed but still the thinnest region.**
🟢 **pt-BR assessment supply EXISTS** — `3` MIT repositories, including a CPU-only scorer whose
phoneme-confusion model is calibrated for Brazilian Portuguese L1 — 🔴 **but every Spanish result
measured carries no licence at all, and the one app built for the actual brief (rural, offline,
indigenous-language-capable) ships no grant.** 🔵 **So `853-G5`'s remedy stands and is now
cheaper: the es-`419` scorer still has to be BUILT, but `gato-zen-speech-eval-zh` supplies a
working L1-parameterised architecture to build it from rather than from a blank page.**

### Global

🟢 **The cross-regional finding of this pass is a research-method finding and it re-prices every
other region on this page: an English-only query channel does not sample a smaller version of this
field, it samples a different one** (`T181`, `0%` vs `100%` yield in the same hour).
🔵 **For a studio the operational consequence is concrete — a client's domain should be searched in
the client's language before anyone concludes the open-source supply is thin.**

🔴 **The second global finding is a licensing one and it cuts both ways in the same pass:
GitHub's `NOASSERTION` field hid a BSD grant on a `5180★` Meta project (`pytorch/executorch`) and
hid a NONCOMMERCIAL grant on an Arabic diacritisation library (`ahmedezz33/compactshakkil`).**
🟢 **A candidate pipeline that filters on the SPDX field alone is wrong in both directions, and
only the bytes resolve it** (`854-G4`).

## 🟢 Fifty-eighth pass (`p853`, 2026-11-16) — **the SPOKEN-LANGUAGE market, placed by region — and the regional legs' real yield this pass is a measured GAP, not a player list**

🟢 **All four mandated regional legs were run verbatim** (`AI education {North America|EMEA|APAC|LATAM} 2026 adoption regulation players`),
plus a tier-specific supply leg per region. 🔵 **The demand-side numbers are in
[`intel/trends.md`](trends.md) (`T179`) so they are stated once; what follows is what a studio can
SELL, and what this base measured that it cannot.**

### 🔴 The informed gaps, stated before the opportunities — because silence looks exactly like coverage

🔴 **The supply legs below returned real results. These returned nothing usable, and that is a
finding about the market rather than about the search:**

| Leg run `2026-11-16` | What came back | Reading |
|---|---|---|
| `spanish pronunciation learning` (first-party `search/repositories`, star-sorted) | 🔴 **`8` results; the best permissive one is `3★` and `NOASSERTION`; the rest are `0`–`1★` with NO licence file.** The only MIT row returned is a commercial vendor's API samples | 🔴 **There is NO permissive Spanish pronunciation-assessment repository.** The Spanish-language learner estate on GitHub is unlicensed hobby apps. 🟢 **`OpenPronounce` (MIT) supports Spanish EXPERIMENTALLY by its own `README` — the only build-safe option, and its calibration language is English** → `853-G5`, **LATAM** |
| `portuguese pronunciation speech assessment` | 🔴 **`0` results** | 🔴 **An EMPTY result set from GitHub's own index, not a thin one.** Brazil is the largest education market in LATAM and there is no pt-BR speaking-assessment repository at all → `853-G5`, **LATAM** |
| `arabic quran recitation pronunciation` | 🔴 **`3` results, `0`–`2★`, every one with NO licence file** | 🔴 **Recitation assessment is a mass-market MEA use case with institutional demand and no open supply.** 🔵 **Named explicitly because the EMEA region in this base is persistently read as Europe; this is the Middle East half returning a measured nil** → `853-G6`, **EMEA** |
| `japanese pronunciation learning app open source` | 🔴 **`0` results** | 🔴 **Nil for the third-largest edtech market in APAC.** 🟢 **Contrast with Mandarin, where the same leg family returned `g2pW` (Apache-2.0) and `OMPAL-corpus` (CC-BY-4.0): APAC's open speech supply is Chinese-published, not pan-Asian** → `853-G6`, **APAC** |
| `accent conversion speech learning` | 🔴 **`4` results, `0`–`2★`, all unlicensed student projects** | 🟡 **Accent CONVERSION (making a learner sound native) has no open supply, while accent ASSESSMENT does. Worth knowing before a client asks for the former** |

🟢 **`5` legs, `5` honest nils, each with the query and the result count attached.** 🔵 **The
pattern across them is one sentence: the open spoken-language estate is ENGLISH-first and
Mandarin-second, and every other language is a localisation project rather than an adoption.**

## Opportunities by region — as filed by `p853` (historical, superseded above)

🟢 **Each subsection names what this pass SHELVED that the region can be sold on, and refuses to
name an opportunity this base cannot supply.** 🔵 **The previous pass's block is preserved below
under its own heading.**

### North America

🟢 **Sell the DEFENSIBLE score.** The regional constraint is evidentiary and legislative —
`~100` state bills touching student AI use in 2026, `≥15` states with enacted or introduced
AI-in-education law, Virginia's `S.B. 394` mandating guidance that covers teacher training, an
NTIA edtech review and a California ballot measure on AI and minors. 🔴 **A speaking score that
cannot be explained to a parent is a liability in this region, not a feature.**
🟢 **What this pass shelved for exactly that:**
[`aalto-speech/interspeech2019_karhila_et_al`](https://github.com/aalto-speech/interspeech2019_karhila_et_al)
(MIT) scores by **articulatorily weighted phoneme edit distance** — every lost point is a named
sound substitution, inspectable without a model card — and
[`TREMA-UNH/rubric-grading-workbench`](https://github.com/TREMA-UNH/rubric-grading-workbench)
(MIT, `p852`) produces the **human-agreement number** a district will ask for.
🔵 **The incumbent casualty is on the record and is the opening line of the pitch: Chegg cut
`45%` of staff as AI took homework-help traffic — the buyers in this region have watched a
market leader lose to the technology and will fund measurement over automation.**
🟡 **Placed rows available: `1` of `12` from this pass's tier (North America), plus the
foundations layer's [`Montreal-Forced-Aligner`](https://github.com/MontrealCorpusTools/Montreal-Forced-Aligner) (MIT).**

### EMEA

🟢 **Sell SOVEREIGNTY, and sell it on the data-residency property rather than on the model.**
The AI Act's high-risk obligations for education moved to **`2 December 2027`** under the May 2026
Digital Omnibus agreement, and the gap between **`76%` of students expecting AI** and **`80%` of
teachers fearing it arrives faster than schools can manage** is the whole opportunity.
🔴 **Minors' voice recordings are the single artefact a European ministry will not send to a
third-party endpoint.** 🟢 **[`Halleck45/OpenPronounce`](https://github.com/Halleck45/OpenPronounce)
(MIT) is the row that removes the endpoint — self-hosted, no API key, learners' audio never
leaves the operator's servers — and it ships experimental French, German, Italian, Spanish,
Portuguese and Dutch.** 🟢 **Two EMEA-placed foundations shelved this pass:**
[`DigitalPhonetics/IMS-Toucan`](https://github.com/DigitalPhonetics/IMS-Toucan) (Apache-2.0,
TTS for `7,000+` languages, University of Stuttgart) and
[`axelspringer/DeepPhonemizer`](https://github.com/axelspringer/DeepPhonemizer) (MIT, g2p from a
lexicon) — 🔵 **together they stand up a European minority-language speaking course without a
vendor: Catalan, Basque, Welsh, Irish, Estonian.** 🔴 **The MEA half of this region returned a
measured nil on recitation assessment (`853-G6`); do not bid it as covered.**

### APAC

🟢 **Sell the SUPPLY, because for once the region publishes it.** The most-starred permissive ASR
toolkit this pass measured is APAC-published —
[`modelscope/FunASR`](https://github.com/modelscope/FunASR), **MIT, `21,049★`**, with punctuation
restoration and diarisation included — and MIT means it can be embedded in a proprietary product
with no NOTICE obligation at all. 🟢 **The only permissive Mandarin grapheme-to-phoneme found is
also APAC:** [`GitYCC/g2pW`](https://github.com/GitYCC/g2pW) (Apache-2.0, Bopomofo/Pinyin,
polyphone disambiguation), with
[`phantomhsieh/OMPAL-corpus`](https://github.com/phantomhsieh/OMPAL-corpus) (CC-BY-4.0, pushed
`2026-11-14`) as the only **licensed** evaluation corpus in the tier.
🟡 **Demand is uneven — `30%` virtual-tutor adoption with connectivity named as the barrier —
which argues for on-device and streaming designs:** 🟢
[`wenet-e2e/wenet`](https://github.com/wenet-e2e/wenet) (Apache-2.0) serves streaming recognition
from a runtime with no Python in the serving path, which is the shape that survives a
`2`-Mbps school connection. 🔴 **Japanese returned a nil (`853-G6`); the APAC supply is Chinese,
not pan-Asian, and a Japan or Korea engagement starts from English-language components.**

### LATAM

🔴 **The honest opening: this pass could NOT find a permissive Spanish or Portuguese
pronunciation-assessment repository, and the Portuguese leg returned an empty result set**
(`853-G5`). 🟢 **Which is precisely why the region's opportunity is the largest on this page.**
The Digital Education Council / Tec de Monterrey survey (Feb 2026, `7,319` faculty, `29`
institutions) reports **`92%` of students and `79%` of faculty using AI with only `30%` of
universities holding a published policy** — a `62`-point governance gap, and **English-language
employability is the region's single largest paid-education category.**
🟢 **What a studio can actually build, from rows shelved this pass:**
[`OpenPronounce`](https://github.com/Halleck45/OpenPronounce) (MIT) calibrated for English with
**Spanish and Portuguese declared experimental**, plus
[`axelspringer/DeepPhonemizer`](https://github.com/axelspringer/DeepPhonemizer) (MIT) to build
the es-`419` and pt-BR grapheme-to-phoneme the upstream projects do not ship.
🔵 **That is a 6–10 week localisation project with a named deliverable — the first permissively
licensed Latin-American Spanish pronunciation scorer — and it is defensible precisely because
this base measured its absence rather than assuming it.** 🟢 **Institutional counterparties for
such work are on record: UNESCO IESALC, UNESCO with Chile's CENIA (including Latam-GPT),
EdTech Hub with Fundación Ceibal, the IDB, and ECLAC's eLAC agenda.**

### Global

🔴 **The global finding is a procurement one and it outranks every regional nuance on this page:
the code in this tier is permissive and the DATA is not.** `29` of `37` grants byte-read this pass
are permissive, 🔴 **while `9` of the `10` rows read for corpora depend on `speechocean762`
(no licence file), `TIMIT` (LDC, paid), `L2-ARCTIC` (request form) or `UASpeech` (signed
agreement).** 🟢 **The one row with no gated corpus —
[`crazycloud/mispronunciation-detection-diagnosis-wav2vec2-and-llm`](https://github.com/crazycloud/mispronunciation-detection-diagnosis-wav2vec2-and-llm)
(Apache-2.0) — is the only end-to-end reproducible pipeline in the tier, and it is not the
most-starred one.** 🔵 **Sell the data-rights review as a priced work package in every region:
it is the step that decides whether a speaking-assessment pilot can ever leave the lab, nobody
budgets for it, and this base can now name the four corpora it turns on** (`853-G1`).


## 🟡 Fifty-seventh pass (`p852`, 2026-11-16) — **the four regional legs run again. The demand side is still saturated; what moved is SUPPLY, and this pass can place it in only TWO regions out of four — which is the honest finding and is recorded as a gap, not smoothed over.**

🟡 **Provenance, stated before the findings, unchanged from `p848`–`p851`.** Every regulatory and

`github.com` HTML answers `403` through the proxy**, so every licence fact on the supply side came
